<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-06", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D01-02", "RF-D01-04", "RF-D01-05", "RF-D01-10"], "n_uids": 2} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D01-02, RF-D01-04, RF-D01-05, RF-D01-10.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/exec/command.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/report.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_replay.rs`, `docs/phase-records/follow-ups-carried.md`, `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp3.md`, `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp5.md`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp3.md`, `docs/plans-archive/R4133_PROPS_PLAN.md`, `tests/TOLERANCE_NOTES.md`
**Findings**
- `RP|RP3.8|SA1|SA-1` (minor) - `exec/command.rs` `refresh_render_caches_for_save` calls `refresh_vterminal_if_marked(ci, oi, None)`; with `prop_idx = None` the `touches_tapnum` arm is unconditionally true and runs `rc.sync_tap_snap_from_live(tr)` for every RegControl, ungated by PropFlags - a fourth kind of render the fn doc denies ("nothing but a marked property's cache moves", "all three marked kinds", "no deck in the corpus ... writes any of these properties"). Behaviour is r4133-correct: doc + pin only. Correct the doc (four kinds; corpus decks do write `TapNum=`) and add a TapNum leg to `save_renders_the_live_result_properties`: deck sets `TapNum=5`, then edits the winding tap; Save must write the live tap (r4133-measured 8 - re-measure the exact deck, see Probes).
- `RP|RP3.11|AC1|AC-2` (minor) - the two `Dump` store-vs-live cells RP3.11 measured outside the echo table (`capacitor.faultrate` r4133 `0` vs port `0.0005`; `autotrans.tap` r4133 `1` vs port live `1.06875`) have no pin, no `PROPS_ECHO_R4133` row and no owner (RP5.2 closed without them). Read the r4133 Dump/property-value source for both cells to decide which serialization is correct; then add an expected-value pin in `exec/tests/report.rs` naming both serializations and register it in `RP311_SERIALIZATION_PINS` (`props_r4133_replay.rs`, guarded by `every_rp311_serialization_pin_exists_and_is_cited`, which asserts the table length EXACTLY - `== 11` at HEAD, message 'the RP5 records publish 11 as an exact count' - and that every pin name appears in `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp3.md`). Because that guard couples the tree and the record in ONE commit, this step makes the single sanctioned exception to the §4 rule: add the new pin's one-line citation to the RP3.11 section of `r4133-props-rp3.md` yourself (that one line only - every other rp3 change stays a note), bump the exact count to 12 (and its message), and update the sentence in `r4133-props-rp5.md` (in Files) that publishes 11 as the exact count. If the port's value proves wrong (R3 applies the other way too: r4133 is the authority unless it is a proven bug), do NOT fix it here - the serialization lives in the Capacitor / AutoTrans property renderers, outside this step's Files: stop that cell, record the r4133 source reading and the port value in `tmp/retro_fix/state/RF-D01-06.md` and the record, and report to the coordinator (owner to be assigned); pin only the cells whose port value is confirmed correct. Write the missing RP5.2 confirmation into `r4133-props-rp5.md` (the four RegControl+AutoTrans decks are `capi_v0145`-only, so no `PROPS_ECHO_R4133` row is missing), add a `follow-ups-carried.md` row if anything stays open, and leave notes for the rp3 record and the plan's "listed in STATUS" claim (R4133_PROPS_PLAN RP5.2 text).
**Probes:** epri-worker r4133: (a) the TapNum deck - `TapNum=5`, edit the winding tap, `Save circuit`, read the saved RegControl line; (b) `Dump` (or `? capacitor.x.faultrate`, `? autotrans.x.tap`) on the RP3.11 decks only where the RP3.11 transcript is missing. Result (a) fixes the literal in the new leg; result (b) plus the source read decides "port correct, pin both" vs "port wrong: stop, record, report" (no renderer edit in this step).
**Acceptance:**

### RF-D01-07 — Port the r4133 HAS_OCP_DEVICE / HAS_AUTO_OCP_DEVICE clear-and-recompute at control recalc
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-07", "effort": "max", "parts": 2, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": [], "n_uids": 1} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** -.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/exec/command.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/element_extras.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/reliability.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/solution/meters/zones/build.rs`, `tests/corpus/ledger.json`
**Doc notes (§4):** `GOLDEN_REBASE_PLAN.md`, `ORPHANED_GAPS.md`, `STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`
**Findings**
- `G|G1.3d(ii)|AC1|AC2` (minor) - `exec/command.rs`: the only writes of `ElemFlags::HAS_OCP_DEVICE` / `HAS_AUTO_OCP_DEVICE` are the two `.include(...)` calls in the control-attach path; nothing ever clears them, while r4133 clears the flags on the controlled element at every OCP-control `RecalcElementData` and re-sets them only when the control is Enabled (Relay.pas:946-964, fuse.pas:465, the Recloser twin). Readers: `solution/meters/reliability.rs` and `solution/meters/zones/build.rs` (feeder-section heads). STATUS / GOLDEN_REBASE_PLAN still defer it to the landed G1.6/G1.6b. Port the clear-and-recompute: because several OCP controls may share one element, recompute the two flags from the live control list (enabled Relay/Recloser/Fuse attached to that element) rather than blind-clearing; cite the three Pascal sites. Pin in `exec/tests/reliability.rs` with an expected-value test: disable the relay -> flags cleared -> the feeder-section head it created is dropped (DG_Prot_Fdr shape), and a second enabled OCP control on the same element keeps the flag. Leave notes closing the STATUS / plan follow-up (or, for anything not finished, a new `ORPHANED_GAPS.md` row - never the landed owners).
**Parts:**
1. Engine port in `exec/command.rs` (+ `zones/build.rs` if the reader must see the recomputed flags at zone build) with the unit pins; gate green; commit. Hand over: which corpus cases could see a moved reliability/zone cell.
2. Run the corpus gate in both lanes; triage every moved reliability/zone cell with an r4133 probe; ledger entries only with an expected-value pin (R8); leave the four §4 notes; gate green; commit.
**Probes:** epri-worker r4133 (and dss-python for the `capi_v0145`-gated rows): on a DG_Prot_Fdr-shaped deck, disable the relay, rebuild meter zones, export the feeder sections / reliability rows - confirms the head is dropped upstream and gives the literal for the pin. A cell that moves in the corpus gate is compared on its gating channel: matches the oracle -> the move is the fix (remove any stale ledger entry, fail-on-stale); diverges -> prove the cause before any ledger row.
**Acceptance:**
- The flags are recomputed from the live control list at control recalc; the disable-relay pin and the shared-element pin pass in both lanes.
- Corpus gate green in both lanes; `tests/corpus/ledger.json` changes only by entries that each have an expected-value pin and r4133 evidence (or is unchanged); no stale entry.
- `lane_diff` run if the solver/zone build path changed (§2.2); no tolerance loosened.
- The uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

Assert the premise from engine state first: read the meter's zone (EnergyMeter branch/sequence list via `exec::view` or the meter state) and require Line.rot and the two loads (oracle: `AllBranchesInZone = [Line.l1, Line.rot]`) to be in it.

- `G|G1.3d(ii)|AT2|AT2-3` (note) - two PhaseLosses citations off by one: `Common/CktElement.pas:1080` for "neutral conductors are ignored by this routine" -> `:1081`; `:1102-1104` for the PositiveSequence x3 -> `:1101-1102` (optionally name the A-Diakoptics twin `:1108-1110`). r4133 `Version8/Source/Common/CktElement.pas`.

Measure the actual gap first, then re-band as `k * scale` with k derived like the 1e-13 band - the new band must be no looser in effect than today's (tighten only; R8). Leave the derivation as a `tests/TOLERANCE_NOTES.md` §G1.3d(ii) note.
- `G|G1.3d(ii)|AC2|AC2-1` (minor) - `report/show/controlled.rs` renders attach order (`derive_control_lists` -> `Circuit::control_attach_order`, r4133 ControlElem.pas:113-131 re-run by every `RecalcElementData`) but no test pins it: both `show_controlled*` byte goldens never re-`edit` a control, so they are order-blind. Add an expected-value pin (in `element_extras.rs`, read-only on `controlled.rs`): two controls on one PD element, re-`edit` the first-created one, assert the literal report line lists the re-attached control last. In `golden_reports.rs` fix the three stale doc comments above `show_controlled_matches_oracle` / `show_controlled_multi_matches_oracle` ("derived `ckt.controls` scan", "creation-order") and state that the capi-generated goldens are order-blind and must never gain a re-edit deck from the capi oracle (capi 0.14.5 never reorders).
**Acceptance:**

- The three citations read `:140/:145/:150`, `:1081`, `:1101-1102`.
- No golden byte moves; no tolerance loosened; notes left for both §4 documents.

### RF-D01-09 — Fix JSON import: per-phase quoted arrays and queued LoadShape/TShape saves
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-09", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D00-13", "RF-D01-01", "RF-D01-02", "RF-D01-07"], "n_uids": 2} -->

Port it now (port-gaps-immediately rule): r4133 Executive/ExecHelper.pas:2860-2881 `DoZscRefresh` - zero the solution `Currents`, and if the active bus index is valid: `AllocateBusQuantities` when `Zsc` is unassigned, then `ComputeYsc(ActiveBusIndex)`; result 0 on success, 1 otherwise (error 274 on exception). Per R3: do NOT adopt the second call. ### RF-D01-12 — Make DeckDirGuard loud: fail on a failed pre-snapshot/removal and take a dir claim (3 copies)
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-12", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D01-02", "RF-D01-11", "RF-D08-08"], "n_uids": 2} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D01-02, RF-D01-11, RF-D08-08.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/tests/harness/deck_dir_guard.rs` (new), `crates/dss-core/tests/harness/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/pd_elements_pins.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_pins.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/reliability_pins.rs`
**Doc notes (§4):** -

Preferred shape: hoist ONE `DeckDirGuard` (+ its directory walk) into the NEW harness submodule `crates/dss-core/tests/harness/deck_dir_guard.rs` (in Files; declared by one `pub mod deck_dir_guard;` line in `harness/mod.rs`, which is 19k lines and is otherwise touched only by `rg`-located edits, never read in full - every harness concern lives in its own submodule file, e.g. `capture_guard.rs`, `run_files.rs`) and make the three pins files use it; if a copy differs materially (`props_r4133_pins.rs` has its own walk, not a `walk_dir` fn), keep the union of behaviours and say so in the record. The model for "loud" is `corpus_gate/runner.rs::assert_swept_clean` and the `sweep_failed` convention of `crates/dss-epri/src/guard.rs`. Start from the `props_r4133_pins.rs` copy as RF-D08-08 (`RP|RP2.3|AC2|AC2-2`, wave 8, in this step's after list) left it - the CorpusGuard contract: no recursion into pre-existing subdirs (or one snapshot shared by nested guards), re-list after removal and panic on a survivor, created directories removed, nested-dir and re-listing self-tests - and hoist the UNION of that contract with the 'loud' additions below; RF-D08-08's self-tests must stay green. This step is the owner of the sibling-copy follow-up RF-D08-08 recorded for `pd_elements_pins.rs` / `reliability_pins.rs`.

**Findings**
- `G|G1.6(i)|AC1|AC1` (minor) - `reliability_pins.rs` `impl Drop for DeckDirGuard` (`let _ = std::fs::remove_file(path);`, same line in `pd_elements_pins.rs` and `props_r4133_pins.rs`): a run-created file that cannot be deleted is left silently inside `tests/corpus/` and reads as pre-existing on the next run, contrary to coordinator decisions D32(2)(b)/D33(2)/D35(2); and the guard takes no exclusive claim although two tests of one binary drive two decks of one directory (`controls/energymeter/`). Fix: in `Drop` collect every failed removal and `panic!` with the list unless `std::thread::panicking()`; serialize same-directory users with a per-directory claim (a process-wide `Mutex` keyed by canonical dir, held for the guard's lifetime). Add a unit test of the guard on a temp directory: a created file is swept, and an unremovable survivor (simulate through an injectable remove fn or a held handle on Windows) makes `Drop` panic.
- `G|G1.6b|AC2|AC2-3` (note) - `pd_elements_pins.rs` `walk_dir` (`let Ok(rd) = std::fs::read_dir(dir) else { return; };`) and the twins in the other two files: a failed pre-snapshot leaves `before` empty, so `Drop` would delete every vendored file of the deck directory as if run-created. Fix: the walk returns `io::Result`; `DeckDirGuard::new` panics when the pre-snapshot fails; in `Drop` an unreadable directory is collected and reported like a failed removal (never treated as empty). Add a test: `new` on a non-existent directory panics. The triage also saw the `let Ok(rd) = read_dir` pattern in `corpus_gate/engines.rs` and `runner.rs`; those files are outside this step - check them read-only and record what you find for the coordinator, do not edit.

**Acceptance:**
- No `let _ = std::fs::remove_file` and no silently-swallowed `read_dir` remains in the three pins files (or in the hoisted helper).
- The guard's own tests (sweep, loud survivor, failed pre-snapshot) exist and pass; all existing pins of the three files stay green in both lanes, run with the default parallel test threads.
- `git status` shows no stray file under `tests/corpus/` after the gate.
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

**Files:** `crates/dss-core/tests/capture_order.rs`, `tools/oracle/oracle_server.py`
**Doc notes (§4):** -

`capture_order.rs` is a source-scanning gate over `tools/oracle/oracle_server.py` and `crates/dss-epri/src/{capture,dss}.rs` (read-only here). Every tightened rule needs a non-vacuity/mutation leg in the style the file already uses. If a tightened rule reds on the REAL sources, that is a true finding: fix it when it lies in `oracle_server.py`, otherwise stop that rule, record it in the state file and report it (the dss-epri sources are owned by RF-D01-14/15).

**Findings**
- `G|G1.10a|AT4|AT4-2` (minor) - `check_run_files_last`: the "classification is the LAST read of the run" clause scans only `src.get(tail_start..scope_end)`, i.e. up to the end of the guard scope (Python `with` block end / the Rust `guard.finish()` line), so a read or file-producing command appended after it inside `run_case` escapes all clauses. Fix: extend the three tail checks (command, `capture_`-prefix read, statement sequence) to the end of `run_case` (Python def end / Rust fn body end); only the documented D32(2)(a) teardown may follow. Add drives inserting a read after the `with` block and after `guard.finish()` that must red.
- `G|G1.3a|AC1|AC3` (minor) - `reads_in` (fed only by `dotted()`): rule 4 ("no unmarked read inside a capture body") sees only dotted `receiver.member` accesses; a read through a free function (`_read_seq(el)`, `read_seq(engine)`) or through a Rust receiver binding not listed in the body's `receivers` needs no marker. Fix: flag undotted calls that take a receiver as an argument unless allow-listed, and treat every Rust binding of the body as a receiver; add two smuggle legs to `the_gate_fires_on_an_unmarked_read` (free-function read; undeclared Rust receiver). If that proves unworkable, narrow the file-head doc of rule 4 and `reads_in`'s "receiver-agnostic" doc to the dotted scope instead - and record why.
- `G|G1.3a|AT1|AT1-2` (minor) - the marked-body sequence builder (`seq` built from `r.markers` only; `capture_group_of(b.family, name)` resolves the group from the marker NAME): a read labelled with the wrong quantity passes once `declared` is edited to match (e.g. a group-B read marked `TotalPowers (A)` admitted before `Losses`). Fix: for marked bodies cross-check each marker name against the read member on its line (Python: member == marker name; Rust: through the existing `snake()` / `expected_r4133_accessors()` mapping plus a declared alias table for helpers) and red on mismatch. Add a mutation test that relabels a group-B read with a group-A name and edits `declared` to match.
- `G|G1.3b|AC2|AC2-4` (note) - the `BODIES` doc paragraph: "the r4133 channel's five bodies" while seven `crates/dss-epri/src/dss.rs` bodies are declared and enumerated. Fix: say "seven" or drop the count.
- `G|G1.8|AC3|AC3-3` (note) - `check_inc_matrix_last`: its anchor loop omits `a.reliability` (which `check_topology_last` lists), and its doc plus the `Anchors` field doc call the incidence capture "the only capture that WRITES solution state" although the reliability capture drives the non-idempotent `RelCalc`. Fix: add `("the G1.6(i) reliability capture", a.reliability)` to the loop, reword both docs, add a drive moving the incidence capture before the reliability capture that must red.
- `G|G1.8|AT3|AT3-1` (minor) - `check_r4133_incidence` / `rust_commands` (needle `exec_wait("`): the "no other executive command" rail sees only literal `exec_wait("...")` calls, and its doc is false at HEAD - `crates/dss-epri/src/capture.rs` drives `engine.relcalc()` (via `command_tolerating_report("RelCalc", ...)` in `dss.rs`) inside the per-step loop. Fix: compare the count of all `exec_wait(` sites with the literal ones (refuse computed commands, as the capi twin does); scan `capture.rs` for the other drivers (`.relcalc(`, `command_tolerating_report(`, `.command(`) and declare the reliability capture's `RelCalc` as the one sanctioned extra site; fix the doc; add non-vacuity legs for a computed `exec_wait` and a stray `relcalc()`.
- `G|G1.8|SA1|SA-1` (note) - the BusLevels overflow citation `DDLL/DSolution.pas:578` (also spelled `:578-582`) in `capture_order.rs` (doc, and error-message strings of the incidence checks - find all with `rg "DSolution\.pas:578"`) and in `tools/oracle/oracle_server.py`: r4133 has `:577` = `ArrSize := length(Inc_Mat_Levels)-1`, `:578` = a trailing comment, `:580` = `setlength`, `:581-582` = the loop/write. Fix: use `:577` for ArrSize and the range form `:580-582`, matching TESTING.md and `crates/dss-epri/src/modes.rs`; re-run the capture_order tests because two spellings are asserted strings.
- `G|bridge D13|AC1|AC-7` (note) - the compared-surface sentence ("exactly `clear -> compile -> post -> n x solve`") in `capture_order.rs` and its twin in `oracle_server.py` (`run_case` doc): since D13 both transports issue `clear -> Set DefaultBaseFrequency=60 -> compile -> post -> n x solve`. Fix: update both sentences.

**Parts:** (`capture_order.rs` is 3.7k lines of ~100 fns: navigate by `rg` on the fn names named above; each mutation leg is one scoped `cargo test -p dss-core --test capture_order` run)
1. The three parser extensions with their mutation legs: tail scope to the end of `run_case` in both transports (`G|G1.10a|AT4|AT4-2`), undotted / free-function reads and undeclared Rust receivers (`G|G1.3a|AC1|AC3` - or its documented narrowing), marker-name cross-check with the alias table (`G|G1.3a|AT1|AT1-2`); run on the real sources in both lanes; commit. Hand over through `tmp/retro_fix/state/RF-D01-13.md`: the legs added, and any rule that reds on the real dss-epri sources (stopped and recorded, owned by RF-D01-14/15).
2. The five doc / rail items: `G|G1.3b|AC2|AC2-4` (BODIES count), `G|G1.8|AC3|AC3-3` (reliability anchor + incidence-before-reliability drive), `G|G1.8|AT3|AT3-1` (`exec_wait` / `relcalc` rail + legs), `G|G1.8|SA1|SA-1` (`DSolution.pas:577` / `:580-582` respell in both files, capture_order tests re-run), `G|bridge D13|AC1|AC-7` (the compared-surface sentence in both files); full gate; commit.

**Acceptance:**
- New legs exist and red on their mutation: read after the guard scope (both transports), free-function read, undeclared Rust receiver, marker-name mismatch, incidence-before-reliability, computed `exec_wait`, stray `relcalc()`.
- `cargo test -p dss-core --test capture_order` is green in both lanes on the real sources; `rg "DSolution\.pas:578"` over the two files is empty.
- `oracle_server.py` changes are comments/docs only - no golden byte, no ledger entry moves.
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D01-14 — Reclassify the five Circuit aggregate rows out of order-free capture group C
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-14", "effort": "max", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D01-13"], "n_uids": 3} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D01-13.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/tests/capture_order.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/capture.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/modes.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/tests/modes.rs`, `tools/oracle/oracle_server.py`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`

**Findings**
- `G|G1.9|AC2|AC2-1` (major) - `crates/dss-epri/src/modes.rs`: the five rows `CIRCUIT_LOSSES`, `CIRCUIT_LINE_LOSSES`, `CIRCUIT_SUBSTATION_LOSSES`, `CIRCUIT_TOTAL_POWER`, `CIRCUIT_ALL_ELEMENT_LOSSES` are `ModeEffect::Impure(..)`, and `ModeEffect::capture_group` maps `Pure | Impure(_) => 'C'` (order-free) - in the module that `capture_group_of` declares the ONE home of the D3 A/B/C mapping - while their own payloads say they reach `ComputeIterminal` (`Common/CktElement.pas:743`), i.e. group A. Fix: make the five rows resolve to group `'A'` while keeping their cursor-moving (re-selection) information: a new `ModeEffect` variant or an explicit group carried by `Impure`; correct the `capture_group` doc. Update the in-module pin `the_capture_order_partition_is_the_one_d3_names` (group A gains the five rows); rewrite `crates/dss-epri/tests/modes.rs::the_five_circuit_aggregate_rows_are_impure` to assert impurity AND group A; if the marker gate in `capture_order.rs` then requires it, add `capture-order:` markers to the aggregate reads of both transports (`capture.rs`, `oracle_server.py`) and keep the positional gate. The at-bus Impure rows (`BUS_ALL_PCE_AT_BUS` / `BUS_ALL_PDE_AT_BUS`) stay order-free by group but keep their LAST-placement contract.
- `G|G1.9|AT2|AT2-3` (note) - `modes.rs` row docs of `SOLUTION_TOTAL_ITERATIONS` ("iterations accumulated over all time steps") and `SOLUTION_MOST_ITERATIONS_DONE`: both misstate the semantics the pin `total_iterations_is_an_alias_of_iterations` settled. Fix: `SolutionI(40)` returns `Solution.Iteration`, the last step's inner count - an alias of Iterations, never an accumulation (r4133 `DDLL/DSolution.pas:218-220`; `TotalIterations` is a local of SolveSnap, `Common/Solution.pas:2678`); `SolutionI(41)` is the largest inner count within the CURRENT step, zeroed by `SnapShotInit` (`Common/Solution.pas:2568`).
- `G|G1.4d|AT2|AT2-1` (minor) - three statements that the capi at-bus twin "is genuinely pure / touches no global cursor": `modes.rs` (doc of `BUS_ALL_PCE_AT_BUS`), `crates/dss-epri/tests/modes.rs` (the at-bus impurity test's comment), `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate.rs` (the at-bus LAST-placement comment). Wrong: capi 0.14.5 `TDSSPointerEnumerator.MoveNext` executes `lst.ActiveItem := currentIdx` at exhaustion (`.inputs/dss_capi/src/Shared/DSSPointerList.pas:184`), so `getPDEatBus`/`getPCEatBus` leave each walked class's cursor on its last element. Fix: correct the three statements and restate the LAST-placement contract as binding on BOTH channels; verify in `oracle_server.py` that the capi at-bus read is placed last too - if it is not, move it and let the positional gate in `capture_order.rs` cover it.

**Probes:** (1) dss-python (pinned 0.15.7): on a small solved deck set a class cursor (e.g. `Lines.First`), read `Bus.AllPDEatBus` / `AllPCEatBus`, re-read the cursor - decides whether the capi cursor move is observable through the API (word the corrected docs from the measurement; the source citation stands either way). (2) If any read order changes in `capture.rs` or `oracle_server.py`, run the corpus gate (it is part of `cargo test`) and confirm no case moves on either channel; a moved value means the old order was reading a stale current - report it, do not ledger it (R8).

**Acceptance:**
- `capture_group_of` returns `'A'` for the five Circuit aggregate rows; `the_capture_order_partition_is_the_one_d3_names` and the rewritten aggregate-rows test pin it; `cargo test -p dss-epri` and `--test capture_order` are green.
- The three "capi twin is pure" statements are gone (`rg "genuinely pure|touches no global cursor"` is empty in the three files).
- No golden byte and no ledger entry moves; a note for golden-rebase.md records the G1.9 classification correction.
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D01-15 — Put every dss-epri read through the mode table: kVBase, CapControlsV sentinel, S replies, errno, Capacity write
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-15", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D01-14"], "n_uids": 7} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D01-14.
**Files:** `crates/dss-epri/src/capture.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/dss.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/ffi.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/modes.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/script.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/tests/modes.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/tests/protocol.rs`
**Doc notes (§4):** - (if a shared doc states the `WP_G1_MODES` row count or the `DO_NOT_CALL` length, leave a note for it; check with `rg` before finishing).

All r4133 citations below are under `.inputs/electricdss-code-r4133-trunk/Version8/Source/DDLL/`. `crates/dss-epri` is test-only and `#[cfg(windows)]`; its `unsafe` discipline (module-level `// SAFETY`) must be kept.

**Findings**
- `G|G1.4a|AC1|AC1` (minor) - `capture.rs` bus capture (`let kv_base = engine.bus_kvbase();`) and `dss.rs` `Engine::bus_kvbase` (`call_f(self.dll.bus_f, 0, 0.0)`): `Bus.kVBase` (`BUSF(0)`) is read outside the mode-table rail - no `ModeSpec`, no `read_mode` shape check, no `check_callable` - while the note in `modes.rs` claims "no capture read bypasses `Engine::read_mode`". Fix: add a `BUS_KV_BASE` ModeSpec (Bus F:0, `DBus.pas:80-89`) to `WP_G1_MODES`; make `bus_kvbase` return `Result` through `read_mode_f` (as `BUS_DISTANCE` does); propagate `?` in the bus capture and in `script.rs`; bump EVERY pinned table count including the capability pin. The input says 111 -> 112 but another audit counted 114 rows: read the count at HEAD, do not trust either number.
- `G|G1.0|AC1|AC1` (minor) - `modes.rs` `classify_v`, `SENTINEL_V_PHRASES`, `V_WITHOUT_SENTINEL`: `DCapControls.pas:313-315` is the one V `else` branch that writes its unknown-mode phrase WITHOUT `myType := 4`, so `classify_v(0, &[])` classifies an unimplemented CapControlsV mode as `Served`; the phrase is listed as covered and `V_WITHOUT_SENTINEL` holds only `"Capacitors"`. Fix: add `"CapControls"` to `V_WITHOUT_SENTINEL` (so `probe_mode` refuses to probe V there) and correct the `SENTINEL_V_PHRASES` doc; alternative, if cheap: decode the `VData::Bytes` reply and match the phrase. Add a unit test pinning `v_sentinel_undetectable("CapControls")`, and the live negative drive of Probes (1).
- `G|G1.0|AC3|AC3-2` (minor) - `dss.rs` `read_mode`, arm `(FfiOut::S(_), ModeKind::S) => Ok(out)`: an `S` reply is returned unexamined, so a family's unknown-mode literal would be handed to the typed accessor as data (the two S rows: `CKT_ELEMENT_ENERGY_METER`, `PD_ELEMENTS_NAME`), although `modes::classify_s` + `S_SENTINELS` exist and `probe_mode` uses them. Fix: run `classify_s` in that arm and return `EngineError` on a sentinel, as the V tag-4 arm does; extend the "Sentinel classification" doc paragraph to S; add a unit test feeding a family sentinel literal. First check that no legitimate value of the two S rows can equal its family's sentinel (record the check).
- `G|G1.0|AC3|AC3-3` (minor) - `dss.rs` `probe_mode` (`let _ = self.poll_error();`) and `tests/modes.rs::every_wp_g1_mode_is_served`: `poll_error` is read-and-clear and the walk pairs probe -> read per row, so the single trailing "the mode walk left errno set" assertion observes only the final phase. Fix: surface the polled errno from `probe_mode` instead of dropping it; in the walk poll after each `read_mode` and assert 0 per row with the row named; reword the closing assertion. Expected green (an audit measured every row errno-clean) - a red row is a real finding to report, not to mute.
- `G|G1.0|AT1|AT1` (minor) - `Circuit.Capacity` (CircuitF mode 0; named only in the `ffi.rs` doc and the `tests/protocol.rs` prose "deliberately NOT driven"): the one state-mutating DDLL mode G1.0 documents is on no register, and the raw worker `ffi` command (`script.rs`, `farg2` defaulting to 0.0) would dispatch it - `DCircuit.pas:193-209` assigns CapacityStart/CapacityIncrement and runs `ComputeCapacity`. Fix: add Circuit F:0 to `DO_NOT_CALL` in `modes.rs` so `ffi_dispatch` and the raw `ffi` command refuse it; bump the pinned `DO_NOT_CALL` length; point the protocol.rs prose at the row; add a protocol pin that `ffi Circuit F 0` is refused.
- `G|G1.0|AT3|AT3-3` (minor) - `tests/modes.rs` `WP_G1_FAMILIES` and the bogus-mode sweep's `assert_eq!(checked, 26)`: the hand-written family list is tied to `modes::WP_G1_MODES` by nothing, and the completeness count is derived from the same list. Fix: derive the set of (family, kind) pairs from `WP_G1_MODES`, assert `WP_G1_FAMILIES` covers it, and derive the `checked` expectation from that set, so a new row from an unlisted family/shape reds the sweep.
- `G|G1.6(i)|AC3|AC3-6` (note) - `tests/modes.rs`, the `// LAST: this phase runs RelCalc and adds elements to the circuit.` comment: false since G1.6(ii) appended `the_bus_reliability_columns_are_live_after_relcalc` after `the_relcalc_protocol_and_the_section_cursor`. Fix: reword to cover both trailing phases (the first runs RelCalc and adds elements; the second reads the post-RelCalc bus columns and must follow it).

**Probes:** epri-worker / r4133 DLL only, through `cargo test -p dss-epri`. (1) CapControlsV with a bogus mode on a solved deck holding a CapControl: confirms the reply carries the phrase with `myType` left at 0 - decides that `V_WITHOUT_SENTINEL` (not a tag fix) is the right register. (2) `BUSF(0)` through `read_mode_f` classifies `Served` and the kVBase values of the bus capture are unchanged. (3) The per-row errno walk: every row 0. (4) `ffi Circuit F 0` is refused before any DLL call (no probe of the mutating mode itself - never drive it).

**Acceptance:**
- `rg "call_f\(self\.dll\.bus_f, 0" crates/dss-epri/src` is empty; `BUS_KV_BASE` is in `WP_G1_MODES` and every pinned count agrees with the table.
- New tests exist and pass: the `v_sentinel_undetectable("CapControls")` pin, the S-sentinel `read_mode` test, the per-row errno assertion, the `ffi Circuit F 0` refusal pin, the families-cover-the-table assertion.
- The corpus gate is green with no ledger or golden movement (the kVBase read path changed, its values did not).
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-16", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D01-15"], "n_uids": 1} -->

Optional, only if it fits: commit the script's census as a fixture that an in-crate test compares with `WP_G1_MODES` (tests must not read `.inputs` at runtime). ### RF-D01-17 — Pin the capi DefaultBaseFrequency prelude, the worker freshness override, the PDElements population and the per-channel re-mask
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D01-17", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D01-11", "RF-D01-12", "RF-D01-13", "RF-D01-14", "RF-D01-20", "RF-D08-05"], "n_uids": 4} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D01-11, RF-D01-12, RF-D01-13, RF-D01-14, RF-D01-20, RF-D08-05.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/tests/capture_order.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/engines.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/runner.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/scheduler.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/harness/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/harness/props_norm.rs`, `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp4.md`, `tools/opendss/README.md`
**Doc notes (§4):** `TESTING.md`, `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`

**Findings**
- `G|bridge D13|AT1|AT-1` (major) - `tools/oracle/oracle_server.py` `run_case` (the per-case `Set DefaultBaseFrequency=60` after `clear`; read-only here): the capi half of the D13 fix has no pin, and `tools/opendss/README.md` claims "Both are pinned by `crates/dss-epri/tests/protocol.rs`", which never touches oracle_server.py; deleting the line silently re-opens a 50 Hz leak on a gating oracle (an audit measured on the pinned dss-python: `Set DefaultBaseFrequency=50` survives `clear`). Fix: (a) a live capi pin next to the D33 worker test in `corpus_gate/engines.rs`: ONE python worker runs a case whose deck sets `DefaultBaseFrequency=50`, then a second plain case, and the second must report 60 Hz (Vsource frequency / circuit base frequency) - deterministic, no scheduler timing; (b) a source pin in `capture_order.rs`, with a mutation self-test like the teardown pins, that `Set DefaultBaseFrequency=60` follows the `clear` in `run_case`; (c) correct the README sentence to name the real pin per channel. The pin must fail, not skip, when dss-python is missing (same rule as the gate).
- `G|G1.6b|AC2|AC2-1` (minor) - the PDElements population "372 / 431 walked cases" (and "96 of the 372", "22 / 29 of ...") repeated in `harness/mod.rs` (two comment sites near `compare_pd_elements` / `PD_SKIP_FIELDS`), `corpus_gate/runner.rs`, `corpus_gate.rs`, `corpus_gate/engines.rs` and TESTING.md (two sites): impossible as a case count (`FORCED_PDELEMENTS_POPULATION` in `scheduler.rs` caps capi-gating cases at 312 + 44 = 356) and re-derived by nothing. Fix: measure walked cases and empty-walk cases per channel from a gate run; pin them fail-on-stale (e.g. a `PD_WALK_POPULATION` constant asserted next to `assert_pd_elements_compare_ran`, fed by the existing walk counters); replace the figures in the five Rust sites by the pinned numbers or a pointer to the constant; leave notes for TESTING.md (both sites) and golden-rebase.md (dated addendum correcting the G1.6b record's "~350 per channel").
- `G|G1.4a|SA1|SA-4` (note) - `corpus_gate/engines.rs` `the_epri_worker_binary_is_not_older_than_its_bridge_sources` (early return when `DSS_EPRI_WORKER` is set) and `epri_worker_bin`: the freshness check abstains under the override. The triage's "consider adding Relay state probes to the four TD21 manifest rows" is outside this step's files (`solvable_now.json`): record it as a follow-up note for the coordinator, do not edit.

**Parts:**

Probe it on both channels (see Probes), register it in `tests/corpus/controls/manifest.json`, append it to `CONTROLS_REQUIRED` in `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/manifest.rs` (RF-D10-01 equality test) and regenerate `population.lock.json` by the documented procedure (`DSS_UPDATE_POPULATION_LOCK=1 cargo test -p dss-core --test population_lock`). Commit; hand over in the state file: the raw event logs of both channels, which lines are DebugTrace-guarded vs unguarded, and any port-vs-oracle difference seen.

Confirm the RF-D09-07-narrowed `expected_eventlog` filter against the deck's traces (extend it or its unit test only for a shape it misses), update the `oracle_parity_cfg_gate.rs` register, run the full gate in both lanes, triage. A port trace line that differs from r4133 is a port bug to fix at its root (relay element sources are outside `files`: stop, record the exact diff in the state file and report to the coordinator - never ledger it without a pin, R8, and never widen the filter back).

Both: the Relay state probes per step.

**Acceptance:**

- The filter in `expected_eventlog` names only the unguarded sample line, with the r4133 citation; its unit test and the cfg-gate register agree; the four TD21 decks stay green.
- The ledger gains no entry; `population.lock.json` moves only by the new case.
- Notes left for golden-rebase.md and TESTING.md; every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-01 — Fix AutoTrans xfmrcode fetch: phases guard, BusNameRedefined, honesty guards
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-01", "effort": "max", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": [], "n_uids": 7} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** -.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/auto_trans/accessors.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/auto_trans/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/auto_trans/windings.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/transformer/windings.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/autotrans_xfmrcode.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_pins.rs`
**Doc notes (§4):** `ORPHANED_GAPS.md`, `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp0-rp1.md`, `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md`
**Findings**
- `RP|RP1.2|AC1|AC-3` (note) - `auto_trans/windings.rs::fetch_xfmr_code` and its twin `transformer/windings.rs::fetch_xfmr_code`: `self.cd.nphases = code.fnphases().max(0) as usize` drops the `If Value>0` guard of Pascal `TDSSCktElement.Set_NPhases` (r4133 `Common/CktElement.pas:365-368`), so an XfmrCode with `phases=0` zeroes the element's phase count. Fix: assign `cd.nphases` only when `code.fnphases() > 0`, in both functions, citing the Pascal lines; pin in `autotrans_xfmrcode.rs` that an XfmrCode `phases=0` leaves the auto's phase count unchanged (add the Transformer analogue in the same test file if it is cheap, else record why not).
- `RP|RP1.2|AC1|AC-1` (major) - `auto_trans/windings.rs::fetch_xfmr_code`: `self.cd.set_nconds(2 * self.cd.nphases)` is a no-op because `set_num_windings` -> `realloc_windings` already wrote `cd.nconds = 2*nphases` raw, so a `xfmrcode=` that changes the terminal shape re-inits the terminals (`set_nterms` -> `Terminal::init`, `bus_ref = None`) without ever raising `signal_bus_name_redefined`; an `Edit AutoTrans.x xfmrcode=<different shape>` after the first Y build leaves all-ground node refs (silent wrong solve or a `Terminal::bus_idx()` panic). Fix: capture `nconds`/`nterms` (and nphases) before the fetch and raise `signal_bus_name_redefined` whenever the shape changed, mirroring the r4133 `NConds :=` setter (`AutoTrans.pas:2353` -> `CktElement.pas:349-361` BusNameRedefined) WITHOUT adopting its buggy `Fnphases + 1` value (`AutoTrans.pas:1021` is the real `2*Fnphases`); correct the function's doc comment, which claims the flag is already raised. Pin: solve, `Edit AutoTrans.x xfmrcode=<code with different phases/windings>`, re-solve -> buses reprocessed, no panic, converged sane voltages. Audit the Transformer twin (`set_nconds(nphases + 1)` after `set_num_windings`) for the same dead-setter hole and fix/pin it the same way if present, or record "twin not affected: <proof>". Leave the record correction as a note for `r4133-props-rp0-rp1.md`.
- `RP|RP1.2|AT1|AT-3` (note) - `autotrans_xfmrcode.rs::no_asserted_cell_can_be_read_off_the_defaults`: the guard tests the proxy "cell differs from a bare auto of the same shape", not the real vacuity condition "cell does not survive deleting its own copy statement"; recalc-derived cells (`rdcohms` when `rdc_specified` is false, tap increment from max/min/num taps - `auto_trans/yterminal.rs`) can differ from the default yet pin nothing. Fix: state the proxy limit in the guard's doc and add a second leg for the recalc-derived cells (compare against an auto built from the same fixture with that one property omitted) or remove such cells from the table with a comment. Do it in the same edit as SA-3.
- `RP|RP1.2|SA1|SA-3` (note) - same guard, `no_asserted_cell_can_be_read_off_the_defaults`: assert_ne-only with no liveness check, so a broken fixture (a property that stops resolving, empty `dss.result()`) passes trivially. Fix: `assert!(dss.errors().is_empty())` after each `Edit AutoTrans.d1 wdg={w}` and after each query, and assert every queried cell is non-empty before the `assert_ne!`.
- `RP|RP1.2|SA1|SA-4` (note) - `autotrans_xfmrcode.rs`, doc of `a_missing_code_logs_100180_and_changes_nothing`: the quoted epri-worker probe `kvs = [115, 69, ]` predates the settlement's fixture move to `kV=138`. Fix: re-measure with the current deck on epri-worker (see Probes) and quote the new result, or, if the worker is unavailable, mark the quote explicitly as taken on the pre-settlement (115 kV) fixture.
- `RP|RP1.2|SA1|SA-1` (note) - `auto_trans/accessors.rs`, the comment citing r4133's Edit loop as `AutoTrans.pas:472`: the `WHILE Length(Param)>0 Do` line is `:474` (472/473 are `NextParam`/`StrValue`). Fix: change the citation to `:474` in the code comment; leave a note for the same citation in `r4133-props-rp0-rp1.md` (record block of RP1.2).
- `RP|RP1.2|SA1|SA-2` (minor) - `auto_trans/mod.rs`, the `bank=` comment ending "see STATUS §RP1.2": the pointer is dangling (records rotated to `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp0-rp1.md`) and the live divergence - r4133 `AutoTrans.pas:519` (`XfmrBank := Param` commented out) + `:566` msg #100130, versus the port's silent acceptance feeding the CIM bank key (`cim/power_xfmr.rs`, following dss_capi `AutoTrans.pas:482`) - has no tracker row and no pin. Fix: repoint the comment at the phase record plus the new rows; pin the port's current `bank=` behaviour in `props_r4133_pins.rs` (accepted silently, stored, readable); leave notes with the exact row text for `ORPHANED_GAPS.md` (owner: undecided adoption of #100130, an L decision outside this step) and `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md` (r4133 lines above as evidence).
**Probes:** epri-worker r4133 only. (a) For SA-4: run the miss test's current deck (kV=138 fixture, `xfmrcode=<missing>`) and read `kvs`/`conns` of the auto; the result replaces the quoted narrative. (b) For AC-1, optional but preferred: on r4133, solve, `Edit AutoTrans.x xfmrcode=<different-shape code>`, re-solve, and read bus names/voltages; it only confirms that r4133 reprocesses bus defs - the port's flag is raised regardless (R3/policy: the `Fnphases+1` value is not reproduced).
**Acceptance:**
- New pins exist and pass in both lanes: XfmrCode `phases=0` keeps the phase count; re-fetch with a different shape after a solve reprocesses buses and re-solves; AutoTrans `bank=` behaviour pin in `props_r4133_pins.rs`.
- `no_asserted_cell_can_be_read_off_the_defaults` has liveness asserts and the recalc-derived leg (or the documented restriction).
- No golden byte moves; `tests/corpus/ledger.json` unchanged.
- Notes left for all three §4 documents; every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-02 — Settle the empty/missed xfmrcode Save surface against r4133
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-02", "effort": "max", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D02-01"], "n_uids": 1} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D02-01.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/autotrans_xfmrcode.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/obj/props/class_props/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/obj/props/class_props/parse.rs`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md`
**Findings**
- `RP|RP1.2|AT1|AT-2` (minor) - `autotrans_xfmrcode.rs::an_empty_code_name_is_a_no_op` (and the miss test `a_missing_code_logs_100180_and_changes_nothing`): the test asserts only "no new error" and `? AutoTrans.t1.XfmrCode == "c3"`, which cannot tell r4133's behaviour (the Edit loop `WHILE Length(Param)>0`, `AutoTrans.pas:474`, stops before property 39 is dispatched) from the port's: `class_props/parse.rs` ObjectRef arm skips the miss/#401 branches on an empty value, calls `set_object_ref(idx, "", None)` and returns Ok, after which `class_props/mod.rs` runs `obj.data_mut().set_as_next_seq(idx)` unconditionally - so Save (membership = the PrpSequence chain, `report/save/save.rs`) can emit an `XfmrCode=` line r4133's SaveWrite cannot. Fix: measure r4133 first (Probes), then either align (no PrpSequence mark when the r4133 Edit loop would stop at an empty Param; on a miss render what r4133 renders) or record the divergence; in both tests assert the Save surface (presence/absence and text of the `XfmrCode=` line in the saved AutoTrans file). Caution: the empty-Param stop is generic parser behaviour for every class and property, and in r4133 an empty Param ends the WHOLE edit (later `name=value` pairs on the same line are not parsed) - an alignment must be scoped by evidence, must not move any golden, and per R8 is abandoned for the record-only path if it would require a golden regeneration or a ledger entry without a pin.
Order inside the step (one part - the probe is four small epri-worker runs and the diff is ~70 lines, so no commit-less hand-over is needed): (1) probe first, before any edit: on epri-worker r4133 run `Save Circuit` after (i) `Edit AutoTrans.t1 xfmrcode=` on an auto that never named a code, (ii) the same on an auto that did (`c3`), (iii) a missed code name, (iv) one non-AutoTrans control (e.g. `Edit Line.x linecode=`), and capture the saved element lines; write the measured facts (commands, saved lines, errors) and the decision "align (scope: ...)" or "record" to `tmp/retro_fix/state/RF-D02-02.md` (the audits and the settler read it). (2) Then decide, following that file: either change `class_props/parse.rs` + `class_props/mod.rs` so the empty-value path does not call `set_as_next_seq` (scope exactly as measured) or keep the code and leave a `DIVERGENCES.md` note with the r4133 evidence; in both branches add the Save-surface assertions to `an_empty_code_name_is_a_no_op` and the miss test, run the full gate, commit.
**Probes:** epri-worker r4133 as in (1) above; dss-python is not an authority here. If r4133 omits the `XfmrCode=` line after an empty edit and the port emits it -> align or record per the caution above; if r4133 also emits it (or Save is identical) -> finding closes with the Save assertions only, recording the measurement in the test doc.
**Acceptance:**
- `an_empty_code_name_is_a_no_op` and `a_missing_code_logs_100180_and_changes_nothing` assert the Save surface and would red if the PrpSequence behaviour flipped.
- The chosen branch is backed by the measured r4133 transcript quoted in the test doc (and in the `DIVERGENCES.md` note if recorded).
- No golden byte moves; ledger unchanged; both lanes green.
- The uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-03 — Tighten prop-flag semantics, compat_quirks pins and upstream-stub controls
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-03", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D02-02"], "n_uids": 9} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D02-02.

Fix: add a `debug_assert!` in `ClassProps::new` that an `UPSTREAM_STUB` row carries no String-arm behavioural flag (`TRANSFORM_LOWERCASE`) - or honour the flag inside the stub arm; extend `compat_quirks.rs::upstream_stub_rows_are_the_measured_set` to assert the carriers' flag set; update the comment above the stub arm.

Fix: add a `HIDE_R4133_GOLDENS` list to `oracle_parity_cfg_gate.rs` with an existence + count (`== 8`) assertion and, if cheap, a check that each artifact contains a carrier class; point the compat_quirks message and the prop_flags doc at it. Re-verify the 8 names against the prop_flags doc before hard-coding.
- `G|G2.6|AT1|AT-1` (major) - `compat_quirks.rs::show_table_layout_is_the_lane_kernel`, claim 2: `assert_eq!(row.starts_with(&pascal), parity)` is vacuous in the default lane (the default row has no comma, so `starts_with(pascal)` is false for any name-column width); the G2.6 settlement `826960c2` replaced the landed bound instead of keeping both. Fix: keep the parity equality AND restore the landed bound `assert_eq!(kw_col(row) >= width + 2, parity)` with the `kw_col` helper (from commit `8f6636f9`; `git show` is read-only and allowed), so a default kernel padding the name column to the declared circuit-wide width reds; update the comment that dismisses the bound; leave a note for the G2.6 settle record line in `golden-rebase.md`. Prove red-first with a throwaway local mutation (not committed): a default row with the name padded to `width + 2` must fail.
- `G|G2.6|AT1|AT-2` (note) - doc of `compat_quirks.rs::device_name_column_is_sized_from_its_content`: claims claim 3 covers "the seven `report::show` call sites" but only `show_bus_powers` is rendered. Fix: correct the doc to ONE call site (Show busflow); the other six lose only padding width, unobservable by construction, as the teardown-register text in `oracle_parity_cfg_gate.rs` already says; leave a note for the same sentence in `golden-rebase.md` (G2.6 record).
- `RP|RP1.1|AC1|AC-2` (note) - `prop_flags.rs` `UPSTREAM_STUB` doc ("no `RecalcElementData`, no Y effect") and `prop_def.rs` `PropDef::stub_message` ("on **every** write"): both hold only for the property's own Edit arm - the class `EndEdit` still runs recalc + `yprim_invalid` (r4133 `generator.pas:746-747`; port `Generator::end_edit`) and `make_like` copies silently (`generator.pas:830-831`). Fix: reword both docs to that scope and cite the pin `generator_neutral_stubs_move_neither_y_nor_the_solution`. Doc-only.
- `RP|RP3.8|AC2|AC2-3` (note) - `prop_flags.rs` `SILENT_READ_ONLY` doc, the "four readers" table: the `ClassProps::get_value` row lists only `?` / `Dump` / `all_properties`. Fix: list all five generic readers - `?` (`exec/command.rs`), `element_properties`/`all_properties` (`exec/view.rs`), Dump (`report/save/dump.rs`), Save (`report/save/save.rs`, via `Dss::refresh_render_caches_for_save`) and batchedit's `where` filter (`exec/batchedit.rs`) - fix the heading count and cross-reference the `RENDERS_LIVE_RESULT` doc. Re-grep the callers of `ClassProps::get_value` first so the list is the HEAD truth.
- `RP|RP1.1|AT1|AT-2` (minor) - `upstream_stubs.rs::stub_rows_are_absent_from_dump_and_json`, JSON half: only `!json.contains(key)` is asserted, so an empty/degenerate document passes (the Dump half has positive controls). Fix: before the negative loop add a positive control per JSON document - Generator JSON contains the object name and a sibling key (e.g. `conn`/`kW`), Sensor JSON contains `s1` and a sibling key (e.g. `Weight`/`kVBase`); use the key spellings the renderer actually emits.
- `RP|RP1.1|SA1|SA-2` (note) - `upstream_stubs.rs::sensor_action_moves_neither_sensor_state_nor_the_solution`: the Y/voltage asserts cannot fail (a Sensor stamps no YPrim) and there is no control write like the Generator arm's `assert_ne!`. Fix: add a control - a real Sensor edit (e.g. `weight=` or `kvbase=`) must move the `state()` table (`assert_ne!`) - and annotate the Y asserts' doc as structural non-discriminators.
- `RP|RP3.7|AC1|AC1-3` (note) - `obj/base/mod.rs`, trait doc of `set_enum_array_raw`: says "the Relay twin keeps the generic arm until B1" while `elements/control/relay/accessors.rs::set_enum_array_raw` exists (same landed commit `82022dab`). Fix: replace the sentence with the truth - Relay implements the hook too (`interpret_relay_state`, `Relay.pas:1237-1308`).
**Acceptance:**

- `stub_rows_are_absent_from_dump_and_json` has JSON positive controls; the Sensor pin has an `assert_ne!` control.

### RF-D02-05 — Stop the corpus guard recursing into sibling case directories (Rust + Python twins)
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-05", "effort": "max", "parts": 3, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D08-05"], "n_uids": 2} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D08-05.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/runner.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/guard.rs`, `tools/oracle/corpus_guard.py`
**Doc notes (§4):** `GOLDEN_REBASE_PLAN.md`, `STATUS.md`, `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`
**Findings**
- `G|G1.10a|SA1|SA-3` (minor) - `corpus_gate/runner.rs`, `CorpusGuard` restore loop (the `Ok(_) => fs::write(&p, data)` content-differs arm; the pre-run photograph is recursive while the classification is not): the sibling-resurrection settlement closed only the GONE arm, so a parent case's guard still writes vendored bytes over a file inside a concurrently running sibling case's directory (11 parent/child case-dir pairs exist, e.g. `Test` -> `Test/AutoTrans`); `corpus_guard_restores_case_dir_recursively` even proves the write happens. Fix: the restore loop must not write back snapshot entries that live under a subdirectory which is another case's directory; derive that set from the manifests (the oracle/epri guards run in other processes and cannot see the in-process claim registry). RF-D08-05 (`G|G1.10a|AC4|AC4-2`, wave 13, in this step's after list) gave this `CorpusGuard` an explicit `finish()` / `assert_swept_clean` sweep-while-claimed path called from `scheduler::run_one_case` - build on that shape, do not re-derive it: a survivor found by `finish()` must never be a file the narrowed snapshot no longer sees (files under another case's directory are neither restored nor reported as this guard's survivors). Add the fixture test `parent_guard_does_not_overwrite_a_running_siblings_file`; keep recursive restore for subdirectories that are NOT another case's directory (adjust `corpus_guard_restores_case_dir_recursively` accordingly, do not delete it). Leave notes correcting the "class closed" wording in the G1.10a record (`golden-rebase.md`), `STATUS.md` and `GOLDEN_REBASE_PLAN.md` (the D30(2) deleted-entry paragraph).
- `G|G1.10a|AC1|AC3` (minor) - `dss-epri/src/guard.rs`, `snapshot` / `restore` (and the twin `_snapshot` + restore path of `tools/oracle/corpus_guard.py`): `snapshot` recurses into every subdirectory and buffers each file <= `RESTORE_MAX` at any depth, and `restore` writes back on the `Ok(_)` content-differs arm, violating the rule `classify`/`classify_into` already state (a file under a pre-existing subdir belongs to a concurrently running sibling case); since G1.10b compares run-file contents this surface is reachable. Fix: stop buffering and rewriting files under a PRE-EXISTING subdirectory - keep recording names, buffer only the case dir's own files; mirror it in `corpus_guard.py`; add a guard.rs pin that a file under a pre-existing subdir modified while the guard is alive is NOT overwritten by `finish()`, and the same case in the Python self-test fixture. Triage sketches differ between the two findings (manifest-derived sibling set for the outer guard vs. "never below a pre-existing subdir" for the process guards): part 1 must pick ONE boundary rule per guard and write it into the state file so all three producers keep one classification; if the simpler "no descent into pre-existing subdirs" rule is provably sufficient for the outer guard too, prefer it and say so.
**Parts:**
1. Read RF-D08-05's `finish()` semantics in `corpus_gate/runner.rs` first (the outer guard's contract the rule must fit). `guard.rs`: write the red-first unit tests (pre-existing-subdir file modified during the guard's life is not overwritten; names still recorded; top-level restore unchanged), then narrow `snapshot`/`restore`. Commit. Hand over in `tmp/retro_fix/state/RF-D02-05.md`: the chosen boundary rule, the new test names, any shared-fixture constants that changed.
2. `tools/oracle/corpus_guard.py`: mirror the rule in `_snapshot`/restore and in `_self_test`; keep the twin constants (`SELF_TEST_*`) in step with guard.rs so `the_python_twin_shares_this_fixture_and_passes_its_self_test` stays green. Commit; update the state file.
3. `corpus_gate/runner.rs`: apply the rule to the outer `CorpusGuard`, add `parent_guard_does_not_overwrite_a_running_siblings_file`, exercise the concurrent parent/sibling scenario, run the full gate, leave the three §4 notes. Commit.
**Acceptance:**
- New pins exist and pass: the guard.rs "pre-existing subdir file is not overwritten by finish()" test, its Python self-test twin, and `parent_guard_does_not_overwrite_a_running_siblings_file` in runner.rs; each was seen red before the fix.
- `a_parent_guard_does_not_resurrect_a_sibling_cases_swept_output` and the Python `--self-test` stay green; the three guards share one documented boundary rule.
- Corpus gate green in both lanes with no ledger or golden change.
- Notes left for the three §4 documents; every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-06 — Harden dss-epri guard unit tests and close Rust/Python twin drift
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-06", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": ["RF-D02-05"], "n_uids": 5} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D02-05.
**Files:** `crates/dss-epri/src/guard.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/src/lib.rs`, `tools/oracle/corpus_guard.py`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`
**Findings**
- `G|G1.10a|AC1|AC1` (minor) - `guard.rs` test helpers `scratch_dir(tag)` and the later `tmp_root(tag)`: fixed machine-wide path `%TEMP%/dss-epri-guard-tests/<tag>` whose first statement is `remove_dir_all`, so two lanes gating concurrently delete each other's fixtures. Fix: make both helpers unique per process/run (join `std::process::id()` plus a nanos or atomic counter) and remove only the directory the test itself created.
- `G|G1.10a|AC1|AC2` (minor) - `guard.rs::the_python_twin_shares_this_fixture_and_passes_its_self_test` is a bare `#[test]` while the Python self-test's D32(2) leak fixture (`SELF_TEST_LEAK_*`, open-file share-mode semantics) can pass only on Windows; the Rust leak twin `a_created_file_the_sweep_cannot_remove_is_reported_as_sweep_failed` is `#[cfg(windows)]`. Fix (preferred): make the Python leak fixture platform-conditional (assert the leak lists only when `os.name == 'nt'`, on POSIX assert the clean sweep), keep the Rust driver ungated, and state in the `lib.rs`/`guard.rs` docs which half is Windows-only. Cheaper alternative if that proves fragile: `#[cfg(windows)]` on the driver test plus the doc note - record which was chosen.
- `G|G1.10a|AT1|AT1-1` (minor) - `guard.rs::restore`, the `Err(e) if e.kind() == NotFound => {}` arm (settlement `728332b6`): no guard.rs test reds when it is reverted; the named pin `a_parent_guard_does_not_resurrect_a_sibling_cases_swept_output` lives in `corpus_gate/runner.rs` and tests a different type. Fix: add a guard.rs unit test (e.g. `a_gone_pre_existing_entry_is_never_written_back`): snapshot a dir with a small pre-existing file, delete it while the guard is alive, `finish()`, assert it stays absent; prove it reds with the arm reverted (throwaway mutation, not committed). After RF-D02-05 only the case dir's own files are buffered, so the "one under a pre-existing subdir" leg of the triage sketch is covered by RF-D02-05's pin - keep a top-level leg here and add the subdir leg only if it is still meaningful. Leave a note correcting the record claim in `golden-rebase.md` (G1.10a settlement) to name this pin for the dss-epri guard.
- `G|G1.10a|AC1|AC7` (note) - `guard.rs::sweep_created` returns survivors in `read_dir` order (iterating `c.roots`) while `corpus_guard.py` returns `sorted(...)`; `restore`'s catch-all `Err(_) => write` attempts a write-back the twin never attempts (`except OSError: pass`). Fix: sort `sweep_created`'s returned names; make the non-NotFound `Err` arm a no-op like the twin (or document the deliberate difference in both twins); pin the order in the shared-fixture comparison.
- `G|G1.10a|AT1|AT1-3` (minor) - drift test inside `the_python_twin_shares_this_fixture_and_passes_its_self_test` (the `py_list` comparisons): it compares `SELF_TEST_PRE_EXISTING`/`RUN_WRITES`/`CREATED`/`SCRATCH` (+ the `AFTER_SWEEP` derivation and the CONTENTS lists) but not `SELF_TEST_LEAK_RUN_WRITES`/`_CREATED`/`_FAILED`/`_AFTER_SWEEP` nor Python `ENGINE_SCRATCH_SUFFIXES`. Fix: hoist the inline leak literals of `a_created_file_the_sweep_cannot_remove_is_reported_as_sweep_failed` into named Rust consts and extend the drift test to compare all five Python constants against Rust consts; touch the `corpus_guard.py` module docstring only if it still overclaims afterwards. The leak consts must be visible off Windows for the ungated drift test (do not put them under `#[cfg(windows)]`).
**Acceptance:**
- `a_gone_pre_existing_entry_is_never_written_back` (or the chosen name) exists, passes, and was seen red with the NotFound arm reverted.
- The drift test compares the four `SELF_TEST_LEAK_*` constants and `ENGINE_SCRATCH_SUFFIXES`; `sweep_created` order is sorted and pinned.
- No test writes to a fixed `%TEMP%/dss-epri-guard-tests/<tag>` path; `python tools/oracle/corpus_guard.py --self-test` prints OK on Windows and its leak half is platform-conditional (or the driver is cfg-gated with the doc note).
- Record note left; every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-07 — Surface epri-worker clear errors instead of a bare cleared:false
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-07", "effort": "xhigh", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": false, "after": [], "n_uids": 2} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/xhigh; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** -.
**Files:** `crates/dss-epri/src/bin/epri-worker.rs`, `crates/dss-epri/tests/protocol.rs`
**Doc notes (§4):** -
**Findings**
- `G|G1.10a F0-prime|SA1|SA-4` (note) - `epri-worker.rs`, the `clear` command handler (`let ok = engine.clear().is_ok();` -> `{"ok":ok,"result":{"cleared":ok}}`): `Engine::clear` (`dss-epri/src/dss.rs`) grew to several strict commands (`clear`, `new circuit.dssrs_bridge_init`, `Set ShowReports=No`, `Set ShowExport=No`, `clear`, `Set DefaultBaseFrequency=60`; r4133 can raise #906/#7000 from `DSSGlobals.pas:793-836` MakeNewCircuit and #301 from `ExecOptions.pas:645-649`), yet every failure reaches the caller as `cleared:false` with no message. Fix: `match engine.clear()` - on `Ok` keep the current reply, on `Err(e)` answer `ok:false` with the `error` text in the same shape the other handlers use; add a `protocol.rs` assertion on the reply shape (success reply has `cleared:true` and no `error`).
- `G|bridge D13|AC1|AC-6` (note) - same handler, same defect seen from the D13 side (the strict `Set DefaultBaseFrequency=60` error text is discarded). Fix: the one code change above closes both; for the error branch add a small test of the reply shape - unit-level on a reply-builder function factored out of the handler if a failing `clear` cannot be provoked cheaply through the DLL. Diagnostic-only: the gate path (`capture.rs::run_case`, `engine.clear()?`) already propagates.
**Acceptance:**
- The `clear` handler no longer uses `.is_ok()`; an `Err` produces `ok:false` plus a non-empty `error` field.
- `protocol.rs` asserts the success reply shape, and a test (protocol- or unit-level) asserts the error reply shape; existing protocol tests that send `{"cmd":"clear"}` stay green.
- No ledger, golden or corpus change.
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-08 — Apply Line.HeightOffset on the coordinate geometry path and fix HeightUnit claims
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-08", "effort": "max", "parts": 2, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D02-03", "RF-D02-04", "RF-D08-03"], "n_uids": 3} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D02-03, RF-D02-04, RF-D08-03; plus `lane_diff.ps1` (§2.2).
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/elements/general/line_geometry/matrix.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/general/line_geometry/tests.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/line/tests.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/support/line_constants/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/support/line_constants/tests.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/ledger.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/oracle_parity_cfg_gate.rs`, `tests/corpus/ledger.json`, `tests/corpus/modes/manifest.json`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/phase-records/golden-rebase.md`, `docs/upgrade/DIVERGENCES.md`

**Findings**
- `G|G2.1h|AC1|AC-1` (major) - `line_geometry/matrix.rs`, `LineGeometryObj::update_line_geometry_data`, the `if !self.equivalent_spacing` loop (`eng.set_y(i, self.funits[i], self.fy[i])`). `LineConstants::set_y` assigns, so the offset applied earlier by `set_line_constants_medium` -> `set_height_offset` is wiped and `Line.HeightOffset` is silently ignored on the detailed-coordinate geometry/spacing path; only the equivalent-spacing branch (`h_off`) honours it. Fix: push `fy[i] + height_offset_m * from_meters(funits[i])` where `height_offset_m = eng.height_offset_meters()` (r4133 `General/LineGeometry.pas:1138`, `+ heightOffset*To_Meters(userHeightUnit)*From_Meters(Funits[i])`; `LineConstants.pas:744-746/759/768` show Pascal's `Set_Y` wipe is compensated there). Add an expected-value pin through the product path in `elements/pd/line/tests.rs`: a `Line geometry=` with `HeightOffset != 0` yields the Z/Yc of the same geometry with the heights shifted by hand, and differs from offset 0; add a geometry-level unit pin in `line_geometry/tests.rs`. Note for the G2.1h record: its claim "the offset lands in the conductor heights that Carson's equations integrate" was true only of the equivalent branch - leave the correction as a doc note. If a corpus case moves, handle per R8/§0 (exclude field-by-field + pin); `ledger.json` is touched only then (capi 0.14.5 has no HeightOffset, so nothing is expected on `capi_v0145`; the one corpus deck with `HeightOffset`, `tests/corpus/modes/upgrade/upgrade_linecs_heightoffset.dss`, r4133-gated, is an equivalent-spacing line, so nothing is expected on `r4133` either - the gate run decides). Any ledger entry this step adds, narrows or retires would carry element `channels`: it carries `measured` (+`source`) provenance taken from the gate run and moves `DEAD_CHANNEL_EXEMPT_CENSUS` in `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/ledger.rs` in the same commit (the RF-D08-03 rule, landed before this step; that file is in this step's files for that constant only). The manifest `note` of that deck's row in `tests/corpus/modes/manifest.json` says "On the detailed-coordinate path HeightOffset is a no-op -- SetY re-sets FY from raw coordinates": true of the pre-fix port, refuted for r4133 by `LineGeometry.pas:1138` - append a short dated correction to that `note` string in the same commit (note text only, no key or population change; if a lock digests the note text, stop per R8 and record it instead).
- `G|G2.1h|SA1|SA-1` (minor) - the doc comment above `LineConstants::set_user_height_unit` in `support/line_constants/mod.rs`, repeated in `support/line_constants/tests.rs` (the G2.1h pin's doc), `tests/oracle_parity_cfg_gate.rs` (the G2.1h citation text) and the G2.1h record. The exclusive claim "the two readings part only after a user `Edit Line.<n> HeightUnit=` followed by a rebuild" is incomplete: `Line::make_like` (`elements/pd/line/accessors.rs`) clones `geometry_obj` (the already-pushed Carson engine) and copies `fz_frequency` but no `height_offset`/`height_units`/`eps_r_medium`, so `New Line.l2 like=l1 HeightOffset=5 HeightUnit=in` reaches the diverging branch with no `Edit`. Behaviour is faithful (r4133 `Line.pas` MakeLike copies no height props either) - fix the claim in every in-scope text (re-locate by the phrase "only after"), add a Line-level pin in `elements/pd/line/tests.rs` (like= copy with a different HeightUnit, rebuild, conductor heights = the typed number under the new unit) so the unit test is no longer "the only cover", and restate the zero-footprint argument (no corpus deck uses `like=` with `HeightUnit` - re-verify by grep). Record text goes to the doc note. `accessors.rs` is not in this step's files: do not edit it. Per R1 also sync the same exclusive claim in the local-only deep dive `investigations/issue-28-line-height-units-reread.md` (MAIN checkout by absolute path; re-locate by phrase - the passage saying the user must change `HeightUnit=` after the fact, ~:92, and the `HeightUnit=ft` example, ~:116): one appended correction sentence in the file's language (Russian) naming the `like=` path; never committed, never moved, named in the record as local-only. RF-D05-07 (later wave) rewrites that file's lane-split description and builds on whatever is there.
- `G|G2.1h|AT1|AT-1` (minor) - `support/line_constants/tests.rs`, the tail of the G2.1h pin (`let before = lc.height_offset_meters(); lc.set_user_height_unit(IN); assert_eq!(..to_bits(), before.to_bits())`). The "no-op change stays a no-op" assertion cannot fail: with the early-return guard of `set_user_height_unit` deleted, `h*(1/0.0254)*0.0254` is bit-identical to `h` for the value used. Fix: find (short python/rust probe) a typed offset/unit pair whose m->unit->m round trip is NOT bit-identical (ft = 0.3048 is the likely candidate), hard-code it, call the setter with the same unit and assert the stored metres are bit-unchanged; prove the assert by temporarily deleting the guard (must go red), then restore it. If no such f64 pair exists for any unit factor, delete the vacuous assertion and say so in the record.

**Parts:**
1. AC-1: the `update_line_geometry_data` fix, the r4133 probe, the product-path pin in `line/tests.rs` and the geometry-level pin in `line_geometry/tests.rs`, the manifest `note` correction, the corpus gate (ledger only if a case moves). Commit green; hand over the probed numbers, the gate result and the names of the two pins in `tmp/retro_fix/state/RF-D02-08.md`.
2. SA-1 + AT-1: the claim rewrite in the four in-scope texts, the Line-level `like=` + `HeightUnit` pin, the no-op-guard probe (guard deleted -> red -> restored), the local-only `issue-28` sync (R1), the two §4 notes, then `lane_diff.ps1`. Commit green.

**Probes:** epri-worker r4133 (`tools/opendss/epri_worker.py`): a detailed-coordinate geometry line, `? Line.l1.rmatrix` / `xmatrix` / `cmatrix` at `HeightOffset=0` vs `HeightOffset=5 HeightUnit=ft` (the audit measured rmatrix `[0.3938131 |0.09365313 0.3955455 |...]` -> `[0.3935408 |0.0933769 ...]`). Decides: the fixed port must move the same way and match r4133 at the calibrated floor; the probed numbers become the expected-value pin. dss-python 0.14.5 has no `HeightOffset` - no probe there. Also run the corpus gate to see whether any case/ledger row moves.

**Acceptance:**
- A product-path pin (Line with `geometry=` and `HeightOffset != 0`) exists, passes in both lanes and reds when the `+ offset` term is removed.
- A Line-level `like=` + `HeightUnit` pin exists; the "only after an `Edit`" claim is gone from all in-scope `.rs` texts; doc notes left for the record and (if a divergence row is needed) `DIVERGENCES.md`.
- The no-op-guard assertion demonstrably reds with the guard deleted (or is removed with the reason recorded).
- No golden byte moves; `ledger.json` unchanged unless a case measurably moves (then each new entry has a pin and `measured` provenance, and `DEAD_CHANNEL_EXEMPT_CENSUS` moves in the same commit); `tests/corpus/modes/manifest.json` changes in the one `note` string only; `lane_diff` Δ stays 0.
- Every uid above is closed or recorded with a reason.

### RF-D02-09 — Align Line spacing/linecode/geometry side effects with r4133 Line.pas
<!-- RF-STEP {"step": "RF-D02-09", "effort": "max", "parts": 1, "gate": "full", "oracle": true, "after": ["RF-D02-08"], "n_uids": 3} -->
**Tier:** executor opus/max; audits, settler per §3. **Gate:** full gate (§2.2). **After:** RF-D02-08.
**Files:** `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/line/accessors.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/line/code.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/line/mod.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/elements/pd/line/tests.rs`, `crates/dss-core/src/exec/tests/line_fetch.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/props_r4133_pins.rs`, `crates/dss-core/tests/corpus_gate/ledger.rs`, `tests/corpus/ledger.json`
**Doc notes (§4):** `docs/phase-records/r4133-props-rp3.md`
Ledger branch (the two ledger files are in **Files** for this branch only; expected untouched): before the gate, grep the corpus for the three changed sequences - a re-`spacing=`/`geometry=` after `wires=`, and `linecode=<unresolvable>` on a spacing line (8 corpus decks define lines with `spacing=` at `ab9f0f76`; expected: no deck exercises them). If the corpus gate still moves a case, exclude the moved cells field-by-field with an expected-value pin: the entry carries `measured` (+`source`) provenance from the gate run and, when it carries element `channels`, moves `DEAD_CHANNEL_EXEMPT_CENSUS` in `corpus_gate/ledger.rs` in the same commit (the RF-D08-03 rule, landed before this step). A ledger entry without its pin is not done (R8).
Existing RP3.6 pins on the very arms this step changes live in `exec/tests/line_fetch.rs` (`conductors_clears_the_linecode_flag_and_the_switch_arm_spares_the_spacing`, cases (1) `linecode=` on a spacing line, (5) `spacing=` without wires, (6) `wires=` after `spacing=`; and `linecode_name_survives_the_flag_that_gates_its_render`, line `g` = the `FetchGeometryCode` site). Their NAMES must survive (RF-D07-10 guards them by `include_str!`); adjust an expectation only where the r4133 probe below is the evidence, and say so in the record.

**Findings**
- `RP|RP3.6|SA1|SA-1` (major) - `line/code.rs`, `Line::fetch_geometry_code` calls the object-destroying `kill_spacing_specified()` (dss_capi 0.14.5 shape, `src/PDElements/Line.pas:1855-1863`). r4133 `TLineObj.FetchGeometryCode` (`Version8/Source/PDElements/Line.pas:2131-2132`) only does `FLineCodeSpecified := FALSE; SpacingSpecified := False;` - the third plain-assignment site besides `:663` and `:696` (`KillSpacingSpecified` exists only at `:687/:692`). A capi bug reproduced in both lanes. Fix: replace the call with `self.spacing_specified = false`, keeping `line_spacing_obj` / `line_wire_data`; add the third site to the `spacing_specified` field doc in `line/mod.rs` (it enumerates only `:663`, `:696`). Pin the probed sequence: `New Line.x ... spacing=sp1 wires=[w1 w1 w1]` / `Edit Line.x geometry=geo1` / `Edit Line.x conductors=[wiredata.w1 x3]` (and the `wires=` variant) -> no error, `? Line.x.spacing` = `sp1`, `? Line.x.geometry` = `` (HEAD errors with "No objects are expected!" / "You must assign the LineSpacing before the Wires Property"). If a `capi_v0145`-gated deck moves, follow the "Ledger branch" paragraph above.
- `RP|RP3.6|SA1|SA-2` (minor) - `line/accessors.rs`, the block-2 guard `idx != SPACING && line_spacing_obj.is_some() && !line_wire_data.is_empty()` and its doc comment claiming it restates r4133's `Assigned(FLineSpacingObj) and Assigned(FLineWireData)`. r4133 `Line.pas:704-713` evaluates the block for property 21 (`spacing`) too, so a re-`spacing=` on a wired line re-fires it including `FRatingsSpecified := False` (`:710`); the port suppresses it, and `Line::fetch_line_spacing` (`code.rs`, `self.line_wire_data = (0..nwires).map(|_| None).collect()`) blanks the wires on every `spacing=`, making the deck fail outright. Fix: include property 21 in block 2, and make `fetch_line_spacing` keep an existing size-compatible `line_wire_data` (r4133 `FetchLineSpacing :1826-1838` never allocates it). Pin the probed deck: `spacing=sp1 wires=[w1 w1 w1] normamps=500` -> `? normamps` = 500; `Edit Line.x spacing=sp2` + `solve` -> no error, `? normamps` = 600 (re-derived from the wires). Rewrite the false equivalence sentence.
- `RP|RP3.6|AC1|AC-3` (minor) - `line/code.rs`, the tail of `Line::fetch_line_code` (`self.spacing_specified = false; self.kill_geometry_specified();`), reached from `accessors.rs` `set_object_ref` only when the reference resolves. r4133 runs the arm-3 side effect (`SpacingSpecified := False; if GeometrySpecified then KillGeometrySpecified; GeometrySpecified := False`, `Line.pas:654-666`) unconditionally - a failed `linecode=` only messages #180 (`:457`). Fix: move the side effect out of `fetch_line_code` into the `linecode=` property side effect in `accessors.rs` so it also runs on a miss. Pin: spacing+wires line with `length=2 units=kft`, `Edit Line.x linecode=nosuchcode` -> the `rmatrix` render scales by Len as on r4133 (`[0.04906667 ..]` -> `[0.09813333 ..]`) while `? spacing` stays `sp1`. Leave a doc note correcting the RP3.6 record's "recorded, not chased" item (i), which describes the miss path as touching nothing.

**Probes:** epri-worker r4133 (DLL 11.0.0.1): re-run the three audit decks (geometry-after-spacing+wires; re-`spacing=` with `normamps=500`; `linecode=nosuchcode` on a spacing+wires line) and record the `?` replies verbatim - they are the expected values of the three pins. If a reply differs from the audit's numbers quoted above, the probe wins; record the difference. dss-python: only to learn whether a `capi_v0145`-gated corpus deck exercises these sequences (expected none).

**Acceptance:**
- Three pins (one per uid) in `line/tests.rs` and/or `props_r4133_pins.rs`, each citing the r4133 `Line.pas` lines above, green in both lanes, each red on the pre-fix code.
- `kill_spacing_specified` has no caller left in `fetch_geometry_code`; the `mod.rs` field doc lists all three drop sites (`:663`, `:696`, `:2131-2132`).
- No golden byte moves; ledger unchanged unless a case measurably moves (then one entry + one pin each, `measured` provenance, census constant moved in the same commit - the "Ledger branch" paragraph); the corpus grep result is in the state file.
